______________

Christ says this on occasion of the Pharisees Charging the disciples for plucking the Ears of corn and Eating on the sabb & Eating when they were going through the Corn fields on the sabbath day. and in the words two things are to be observed 1. how the pas X cites a passage in the passage that X cites out of the O. T. & 2. How X applies it.

1. H I would take notice of the passage it self that is cited from the O. T. I will have mercy & not sacrifice The passage is cited from the Prophecy of Hosea. the 6. Chap. 6. v. For I desired mercy & not sacrifice and the Kn. of G. more than burnt offerings tis only only the former part of the v. that is quoted by X in the Text. in which passage may be observed two things

_____________

1. Two kinds of duties Compared mercy & sacrifice the one One a moral duty towards men viz that of mercy. for G. in the place cited is speaking of the things that he expects of men and what duties he Chiefly insists upon in them from them & he de says he desired mercy. that is he insists on their doing acts of mercy one to another. [^001-note1] The other viz sacrifi is an a is a duty of External Relig. towards God. viz. offering sacrifice. This was a part of Gods Instituted worship External w. it was so then when the Prophet Hosea said it & it remained to be still when X cited the words as is writ as we have an account in the text for the Law of sacrificing & the rest of the Cer did not cea & the Rest of the Ceremonial Law did not Cease 'till d Xs death & Resurrection.

______________

so that the duties that are here compared Kinds of duties that we have Compared together are moral duties towards men & Extern the duties of External worship towards G.

________________________

2. we may observe to which of these the preference is given viz to that which is a moral duty towards men who prefer this this G. Prefers before sacrifice that is an external duty of Religion towards God.

_________________________

nextly we may observe how X applies this viz to Justify his d. in doing that [--] for their own relief when in want that otherwise would have been a breach of the sabbath for by the Law of Moses it was unlawfull for persons to so much as to Gather sticks to dress their food [^001-note2] or to gather their food on the sabbath & theref. they might gather no manna in the wilderness on the sabbath. & when some of the Children went forth on that day hoping to find manna it was Rebuked as a Great sin gathering manna is spoken of as Contrary to that rest that G. then required on the sabbath Exod. 16. 23.

__________________

X seems to allow that their plucking Ears of Corn & rubbing them in their hands to get food to eat on the sabbath day would be unlawf. were it not that their own relief required it. he allows that that were it not for this it would be a profanation of the sabbath. & he brings Justifies by the Exam Just other Instances of Like nature allow that in that simply considered void of their circumstances are profanations of the sabbath & yet are spoken of as allowed of in SS. as the Instance of the Priests in the Temple Math 12. 5. as v. 5. ------------------------------------

doc. Moral duties towards men are a more Essential part of Relig than external acts of w. Worship towards G.

Tis not only that particular moral duty towards men viz. shewing mercy to em that is a more Essential part of Relig. than that particular act of w. ------ viz. sac offering sacrifices

But it appears by Xs application of this Passage of the prophet Hosea that the preference is given to moral duties towards men to acts of mercy not only before sacrificing but to other external duties of Gods worship. for X applies it here to an external keeping of the sabbath. which is a part of Relig that belongs to Gods External W. des that is as much insisted on in SS. as any part of the External worship the Command in which it is Required is not one of the Ceremonial but one of the ten Commands one of those Great Commands that were given at Mt. sinai by an audible voice & that [^001-note4] were written on Tables of stone & X in the Context also mentions another duty part of Gods External worship viz. that sacred regard to the Temple & to the holy things of it which the Law Required & which david & those that were with him violated when they were in distressing want. These things shew that tis not meant only that particular part of w. fo sacrificing but that is meant but any part of the Ex outw. w. of G. indifferently

### 541. Tit. 2:14(a)

### 541.Tit. 2'14a(3_40) edited

Jonathan Edwards

______________________________________________________________________________

Tit. 2. 14. Who gave hims. for us that he might Redeem us from all Iniq. & purify unto hims. a peculiar People zealous of Good works.

_____________

I have already in discoursing from these words obobserved several things in them which there is no need of my repeating. what I now Intend from these words Leads me especially to obs observe two things three things in em.

__________

1. a Certain vertue or Good Qualit Quality. here mention. viz a being zealous of Good works. &.

_________

2 we may take notice that this vertue is mentiond as a distingui part of the description of those that belong to Xs mysti Invisible Chh. the Peculiar People of the People that are Redeemd from all by X from all Iniq. and X purifies to hims. & that are d are his peculi ar people are decypherd by this that they are zealous of good works this is here mentioned as part of a main part of their Character . & a that by which they are distinguishd as a peculiar People from others 3. I would observe how much X has done [^009-note1] that they might his People might be endowd with this property or qualification . He gave hims. for them to this End. so much did so Great a Regard had he to this that he such a vertue qualificaiton in his Chosen People so much did he seek to have a people of such a Character that he gave hims. offerd up hims a sacrifice to div. Justice to make way for their being Possessed of it. Theref. the doc. that I would now observe from the d words is this.

________________________________________________

doc. Zeal is an Essential vertue of a Xtian.

______

This is evident from the Text because by that it appears that it is part of his descrip in the Text it is mentioned as part of the descripwhat belongs to description of a true Xtian Xtia & part of his distinguishing Character . & also because tis as what that mentiond as a vertue that X purchased for Every one of his Elect & not only so but one Great they that he aim & main thing that he aimd at in that pure sacrifice which he made of hims. whereby they were purchased . I would handle this point . [^009-note2] To Clear up this point I would 1 she describe this vertue or shew what it is. 2. would give the Reasons why we are to Look it is to be Look'd upon as an Essential vertue of Qualification of a Xtian .

_______________________________________

I. I would shew what is true Xtian zeal. Christian zeal is an a fervent disposition an affection of mind to prosecute in prosecuting those things that tend are for the Glory of G. for are pleasing to G. & for God's Glory & in opposing those things that are against it.

___________________

This affection or disposition of mind is called zeal principally from the its fervour or ardency whereby tis like an Inward heat or flame in the soul. It might have ' defind in one word to be a zealous more briefly a fervent fervent disposition or affection of mind that G. may be pleased & Glorified. for G. is the princi Gods pleasure & Glory are the principal object of the Exercises of this vertue: & placed next to that h and next to that the means of pleasing & Glorifying G. either the Removal prosecution of those things that leads to or the Removal of the hindrances of it . next p. but one But to ha at give a more distinct notion [^009-note3] zeal is a word often used & persons are often spoken of as being zealous persons & very Commonly tis understood in an ill sense. tis

________________

& su & such an Idea is very commonly annexd to the word as carries in it weakness & pride. superstition & anger & probably most Persons have no distinct Idea notion of of what is meant & the notion that they have is one a disadvantageous one the word is so often used to signify some weak & improper vehe Heat & vehemence of mind. that it scarcely adds any thing to the Goodness of any persons Character to hear it said of him that he is a zealous man but rather detracts from it. But the yet we from time to time read find zeal Recommended in SS. as and spoken of as a Xtian Qualification & that zeal whatever notion disadvantageous notions w persons may have of zeal through the abuse of words yet that zeal which the SS. recommends is indeed a noble & Excellent Qualification to be zealous in this sense is to be one of an amiable & divine Character. & tis one thing wherein the Beauty of Excellency of pure Xtian vertue does very [^009-note4] much appear it has nothing unlovely or unsuitable in it & is not only ver very Commendable but Essential in a true Xtian.

_______________________________________________________________________________

. But to give a more Clear & distinct notion of this Excellent Quality of mind. I wou[l]d more particularly describe it by shewing what affections are Exercised in it and the manner of their Exercise & the acts in which this this vertue Exerts it self.

______

as to the first viz The affections that are Exercised in this in which the true Xtian Zeal has its seat or which are Exercised in it

________

1. That affection that is principal in this vertue is Love. Zeal is an Inward heat or fervency of Sp. & Love is the flame whence that heat comes. tis This is the fire that fills the soul with that holy fervour that is called zeal & those other

__________

Love to G. & X & this divine Love divine Love is the founda. of all those other affections that are exercised in Xtian Zeal. tis Love is an active princi divine Love is an active principle it is fire from heaven may be calle compared to an holy flame Kindled in the soul by a beam st from [^009-note5] thence no man is truly zealous without it it is the sp. that animates & actuates & animates the truly zealous man. He that is zealous from any other principle than Love his Zeal is no Xtian Zeal it is for of a spurious Kind & is a vi no true vertue but a lie it is nothg amiable bu it is a vice & not a vertue The Psalmist Expressed a Great Love to G. when he said . Ps.119. 139. my zeal hath Consumed me because mine Enemies have forgotten thy words & so did Phinehas in that act of which G. speaks in Numb. 25. 11. 12. 13 ------------------------------

______________________________________________________________________________ _____________________________________________________________________________

2. from Love to G. arises a desire that he may be Glorified . one way in which Love is exercised in zeal is desire from desire of that this G. that is so beloved may be have pleased that his will may be done himse Glorified that his Commands may be obeyed . & that his name may be Glorifie that hes name may be feared that he may [he was his] be Loved that men may be holy & he is holy & that his Kingd. may be advanced. [^009-note6] & that Every thing that is against these things may be Removed. The Apostle Joins fervent desire & zeal together in 2. Cor. 7-11. yea what vehement desire yea what zeal. in zeal an aversion to

______________________________________________

3. There is an Hatred of Those what is contrary to Gods command & Glory. from that Love which is the principal thing in Xtian Zeal & the founda. of all the Rest that is in it arises Hatred. There is both Love & Hatred exercisd in Zeal The Love of G. & Hatred of sin. Hatred of those things that G. hates a hatred of what tends to be the is against the Interest & reputation of Relig. & against or opposite to the flourishing of Xs Kingd & opposes the flourishing of Xs Kingd.

________________________________________________________________________

4. another affection that in is Exercise[d] in Xtian zeal is a sp. of Jealousy for G. zeal & Jealousy in the original of the N. T. are the same word He that has a sp. of true Xtian zeal is Jealous for the Cause of X & if it If it at any time that Cause be injured a sp. of Jealousy is excited in him . when & when the Interest of Relig. is touched his heart is touched his & he is mocked & alarmed . 5. another thing affection that is exercised in [^009-note7] true Zeal is courage a sp. of fortitude in Enervating the difficulties that are in the way of doing Gods will , & the Enemies that oppose his Glory.

______________________________________________________________________

2. As to the manner wherein these affections are Excercised in true Zeal it is with ardour or fervency of sp. That divine Love which is the main thing in Xtian Zeal is a Powerfull principle tis a fire Kindled in the soul by the active sun beams of the sun of Righ.

That desire that there is of the Glory that Gods shewd his fain Glor pleased & J done & his [shou[l]d?] name Glorified is no faint d but an ardent desire that aversion that there is to

sin in that hatred there is to sin in Xtian Zeal & the aversion there is to whatever is against Gods Glory is an has a vehemence it that Jealousy by which they are Jealous lous for the L. of Hosts is also an ardent affection that alarms the soul & awakens its active principles there is also a fervour of and in those holy fortitude & Courage that is excercised in the Heart of him that is truly zealous with a truly Xtian Zeal. 3. as to the acts in which in which this vertue [^009-note8] Exerts itself. They are of two Kinds in one it is in pursuing the Glory of G. tis not all holy fervency of Sp. that is properly intended by Zeal but it is that fer a holy fervour of mind as it Relates to practice only or the persuit of the Glory of G. & that Interest as those things that are wellpleasing to him. There are many examples of fervent Love to G. that true Love to G. & fervent Love to G. that are dont properly com come within the signification of this Term as for instance Love to G. as exercised in delight & Complacence & Contentmt in him in Rejoicing in a view of Glory of his Glory or communion with him & tasting the sweetness of his Love. these things are not properly called Zeal. But tis Love with Respect to that Exercise of Love the it that is called Zeal that there is in pursuing his Glory the a Kingd. that is called Zeal. so tis not all de ardent desires & truly

ti so tis not all gr truly Gracious & ardent desires after G. --------- after the Enjoy mt of G. ----- but tis an ardent desire to do something whereby G. may be Pleased -- [^009-note9] & honourd. so the hatred that is in Zeal is of sin that is in Zeal is Exercised in opposing it & the Jealousy for Gods Honour in a that is properly comprehended in Zeal is Exercised in opposing what is o sin & that which is opposite to Gods Glory. So the Courage & fortitude that is in Zeal Relates to action that action that is in pursuing Gods Glory.

& these acts that zeal her is excercised in are of two Kinds They are either a Persuing 1 a Prosecu a Prosecuting those things that which tend to promote Gods Glory. as when a Person has a fervent exercises a fervency of sp. in doing Good works or ourselves in loving G. & Glorifying G. our selves or as to promote such things in others which appears in an ardent disposition of mind both to in promoting not only the worship of G. but also the Good of men in one word a fervency of sp that Good may be done for Gods & Xs sake whi either to do it our selves or that others may do it [^009-note10] to promote others doing of it whether this good be what Immediately Respects the Honour of G. or nextly concerns the Good

II. Place That is an Essential vertue of a Xtian. H insomuch that not only is it [-] a true zeal a noble attainmt of a Xtian but he is bu he a Zealous Xtian but he [^009-note11] that is not a Zealous Xtian is not allowed in SS. to be any true Xtian. for.

1. This is the Qualification principally by which true Xtians are distinguished from those that are Lukewarm. The SS. distinguishes mankind into three sorts those that are Cold who are those that appearance or shew of Relig. and are not to be Looked upon as visible Xtians . & those that are hot i.e those that are true Xtians that are fervent in sp. in sp. serving the L. that have within them that holy ardour & Engagedness of mind in Relig. that has been spoken of & those that are lukewarm that are that is those that make a profession of Relig . & have some shew of Relig. but are not thorough in it are cold & lu dull & lifeless are an Indifferent sort of Professour have no Great Concern about the Honour of G. & Interest of Relig. who who are not true Xtians and never will be accepted of X as some of his if they continue for we may observe that of all of those three those [^009-note12] that are most abominable to X are those that are Lukewarm that bent zealous Xtians . Rev 3. 15. 16. ------------

The Lukewarm Xtians are more hatefull to X than the openly profane than the very h Heathen that make no pretences to Relig. X declares expressly that he had rather men should be quite Cold than only Lukewarm

I would says he that thou were cold or hot as the stomach nauseates Luke warm water more than either cold or hot so doth X abominate Lukewarm Xtians that are destitute of true Xtian Zeal in Relig.

2. This is one thing wherein the Power of Godliness appears & wherein it differs from the form of it. These two are spoken of in by the apostle in 2. Tim. 3. 5 [-] The one as being Peculiar to the saints true Xtians, the other Common to them Hypocrites with them . sincere

_______________________

acts sincere Grace is a Powerfull principle in the soul. and the Power of it appears partly in the nature of its actings it is no [^009-note13] dull inactive ineffectual thing . There is an Holy ardency & vigour in the actings of Grace It is Like a Kind of inw. sp. fire in the soul & there & theref. when a man is converted & G. pours out his H. sp. upon him he is said in SS. Language to be baptized with the H. Gh. & with fire There is an inexpressible ardour of soul when true Grace is in exercise so that the hearts of the saints do as it were burn within them. Luke 24 32 did not our Hearts burn within us while he talked to us by the way & --------------- and This Holy ardour & Power that is in the Exercises of true Grace rises not in in men by contemplation & Enjoymt but has Respect to practice it seeks the Glory of G. it struggles after those means of means that t tend to promote it & aft against those things that do oppose. & so it has the nature of zeal.

___________________________

The tru He that experiences the Power of Godliness at times finds within hims. ardent and gr breathings & Longings of soul not only after a si more of a sight of G. & more of an Enjoymt of him but also holiness & Con- [^009-note14] formity to G. such as have a strength & a Conin them & such a peculiar sort of Inward vigour as other desires have not They sometimes cause the soul to pant after G. & even to strug and occasion a vehement struggle against sin

______________

& Even in them that have Comparatively small degrees of Grace yet G. is wont sometimes so far to quicken the principle as to Let the Powerfull nature of it appear in some measure

______________

There is something in the vigour of the actings of true Grace that is inimitable & inexpressible that does properly shew the almighty that there is an omnipotent agent at work in the soul of a Godly man. This makes true Xtians zealous of of Go to in pro prosecuting those things that tend to Gods Glory & opposing what is against wheras others that have only the form of Godliness without the Power of it are in men indifferent Lifeless & Lukewarm with Respect to these things

There is no other disposition an Indifferent & Lukewarm sp. in divine does in no wise become the nature of divine objects divine [^009-note15] objects are infinitely Great Immensely surp surpassing all temporal things in their Importance & Excellency . when G. gives men true Grace & Rightly disposes their hearts with Respect to divine things he will give em such Kind of dispositions & affections towards them as do in some measure become their[-] nature & Importance . Hence X says he he that Loves F & mother more than him is not worthy ------ [see serm. Matt. 10:37] He that Loves son or daughter ----- He that takes up -------------- that is he that has no other affection towards him than such an one as falls below or at Least or at Least is not above his affection to sp any temporal objects or Enjoymts his affection & Respect is in ways becoming its object & so will never be accepted . & theref he says he elsewhere that he that comes to him & hates not his F Luke. 14. 26. ------------------

But here a Question or Objection may arise which I will now take occasion to speak[-] some thing to. The Objection. [^009-note16] OBJECTION is this viz. How can it be necessary that G men in order to a being true Xtians should Love G. above all things else in the Θwhen we are so often taught that true Xtians have so little Grace & so much Corruption . we are often taught that Grace in this Life is but an an Infant state that Grace is but as a spark faith but as a Grain of mustard seed but that men carry about with them a Load of sin a body of sin & death his corrupt pri corruption which is so Great inclines him to Love the Θ & make that his happiness & if there be so much of it & so little Grace how can it be that a man should have all truly Godly men should Love G. more than the Θ I answer ANSW 1. Tis from the nature of the object Loved rather than the degree of the principle in the Lover . The object beloved is of supream Excellency of a Loveliness Immensely above all worthy to be chosen persued & Cleaved to above all & delighted in far above all & he that truly Loves him Loves him as seeing this superlative seeing of it as superlative & as being Convinced that it is far above all tho a man has but a faint discovery of the [^009-note17] Glo. of G. yet if he has any true discovery of him so far as he is discovered he sees this he is sensible that he is worthy to be Loved far above all The sp. of G. is a sp. of truth & if he makes any true discovery of G. it must be a discovery of him as Lovely above all if such an Excellency is not discovered there is no divine Excellency discovered for the notion of divine Excellency is superlative supream Excellency

_______________

now that wherein a Godly m. may be said to Love G. above all seems to be built seems to be no more that immediately & nec than what Immediately & necessarily follows from a sight of this supream Excellency tho it may be comparatively a faint discovery of it tho it may be a comparatively faint discovery yet tis a Convincing discovery Hence G. must be above all in his Esteem. for to be Con vinced that he is more Excellent than all is to Esteem in fact to Esteem him above all

___________

& so he must be above all in his Choice for the Choice follows the Esteem & hence also it will follow that G. is above all in his purpose & Resolutions he Cleaves to the Lord with purpose of Heart & so in the sense of the SS. with his whole Heart [^009-note18]

____________

Tho there may be but Little of the principle of Love yet the principle that there is being built on such a Conviction will be of this nature viz to prize G. above all There may be an Endless variety of degrees of the principle but the nature of the Object is unalterable theref. if there be a true discovery of the object whether in a Greater or Lesser degree yet if that discovery be true & agreeable to the nature of the Object The nature of the principle that if is the Effect of the discovery will answer the nature of the object & so it will ever more be the nature of it to prize G. above all tho there may be but little of such a principle.

___________

2. another way whereby Grace is predominant in the soul of a saint is by vertue of that Cov. of Grace & the promises of G. on which Xtian grace Relies & which Engages Gods strength & assistance to be on its side & to help it against its Enemy which would otherwise be overpowerd

where G. infuses Grace he will give it a predominancy by his upholding of it & time after time giving it the victory when it seemd for a time b to be overborn & ready to be swallowed this is not owing to our strength but [^009-note19] the strength of G. who wont forsake the w. of his hands & will carry on his w. when he has begun it & alwaies causethhis us to triumph in [was cause his] X Jesus who is the Author & has undertaken to be the finisher of our faith.

### 542. Matt. 14:12(a)

### 542.Matt. 14'12a(4_40) edited

Jonathan Edwards

On Occasion of the funeral of Rev. Nehemiah [later xo] Bull of Westfield appointed April. 14. 1740.

______________________________________________________________

Math 14. 12. and His disciples came & took up the body and Buried it and went & told Jesus in concerning these words several I would observe 3 things 1 on what occasion that was which we have an account of in the text

___________________________________________________________

we have an account in the Context of death of John the Baptist who was cruelly murderd By Herod at the Instigation of Herodias having exposed hims. to their malice by fai his faithfully Reproving them for their wickedness Incestuous wickedness In the Text we have an Account of the behavior of him John something that was consequent on his Consequent - & therein may be observed two things

_____________

1. who are the persons here spoken of even those disciples of John from that was no that had been the disciples of John the Baptist that was now dead who has was a person whose business it had [been] to [E om.] Preach the Gospel of the Kingdom of G. He was a minister of Jesus X and had been Improved by him to do Great service he was an Instrument of much to Good in to many in Judea & Jerusalem in his Life time . what we have an account in the text was Consequent on his death

_________________________________________________

2. we we may observe who are the persons spoken of [in] the text they were those that had been the [E om.] disciples of John the Baptist those that had been [^010-note1] his followers There is nothing in the accounts the [later bracket] SS. give us of him that Leads us to suppose that he had Left any widow or natural Children But these were as it were his Chil. his followers [later bracket] followers his Constant hearers that were wont to sit at his feet & hear him preach the Gosp. that were his constant followers that were with him & being as it as those that Recieved Great Benefit by his ministry and were as it were his Children.

________________________________________

3. we may observe their Behaviour on this occasion. consisting in[-] two things 1. Their that whereby they shewed their regard to the remains of the deceased by giving it a decent Burial They took up the Body & Buried it. it had Used in a Barbarous Manner by others that had also been his bearers and were under special obligations to have treated him with honour they Cruelly murderd him by severing his head from his body and his head was carried in a Charger to Herodias that she instead instead of Paying that Respect that was due to the Remains of so venerable a person might have her malice & cruelly gratified by such a spectacle & that she might thence take occasion to Insult the dead. that part of the dead bo while that part of the dead body was thus used by He- [^010-note2] rodias the his disciples out of Respect & honour to the memory of their master & teacher decently Inter the Rest.

______________________

2. That which they did for Gods Glory & their own in Conseq. on this for Gods Glory & their own Good they went & told Jesus Jesus they Knew to be one that their master John while he lived had testified a Great regard to He X as John was he whose forerunner John was & whom he had preached of whom he had said Behold the Lamb of G. that taketh away the sin of the Θ & this is he of whom I said after me cometh after is preferred before me and whom he saw and bare record that this is the son of G. and Probably they Knew that X was one that had put Great honour upon John their teacher in his Life time for he had tho he was the son of G. & John Johns maker & saviour yet came to him to be baptized of him & had said of him that among those that were born of woman there had not risen a Greater than John the Baptist. It was now a sorrowful time with Johns disciple when they saw they were thus bereaved of their him that whose teachings they had sat under & the manner of his death was doubtless very Grievous to him they were like a Company of distressed sorrowful Children . & John is the [^010-note3] & what do they do in in their sorrows but Go to Jesus with their Complaint their f the first thing they do after this after paying proper Regards to the remains of their dear teacher is to Go to Jesus & to spread their Case before him seeking Comfort & help from him & thus they sought their own Benefit by it & pr probably one end of their Immediately Going & telling Jesus was that Jesus being informed of it might Conduct hims. accordingly as as his wisdom should direct for the Interest of his own Kingd.

th when so Great a person as John the Bap. had been was thus mar the forerunner of X was thus martyred it was a Great Event much Concerning the Common Cause in which both X & he were Engaged. & accordingly Jesus is Immediately Influenced in his Conduct by the Tidings as you may see in the next v. --------- --------- Thus they sought Gods Glory in it

doc. when any is taken away by death that has been improved & made serviceable in the work of the Gospel tis suitable that such as b are bereaved thereby should Go & spread their Calamity before Jesus.

Let us Go to Jesus & seek Grace of him that we may be faithfull while we live & theref he would assist us in our Great work that we may when we also are Called hence we may give up our account with Joy & not with Grief & that we hereafter may appear meet those our Fathers that have sai gone before us in the faithfull Labours of the Gospel & that we may with them hereafter shine forth with as the brightness of the firmament & as the stars forever & Ever.

### 544. Heb. 12:22-24(a)

### 544.Heb. 12'22-24(4_40) edited

Jonathan Edwards

April 1740 N/I.. (Northampton, History of Redemption reference) Heb. 12.22.23.24. But ye are come unto mt Sion and unto the City of the living G. the Heavenly Jerusalem ------------------- -------------------------------------------------------------------- 32 The apostle had in some of the preceding verses been Ernestly warning the Xian Hebrews or Jews carefully to avoid the ways of sin. 13 v. & c. and the Great argument that the apostle makes use of to Enforce this ----counsel is that they were not come to mt Sinai as their Fathers were of old but to mt Sion the Heaven ly Jerusalem & c ------------------- Signifying that there is more at mt Sion to Restrain us from sin than there was at mt Sinai ~~ There was a Great deal to Restrain from sin that their Fathers saw and heard when they came to mt Sinai. There was mani fested the terrible majesty of G. for the mt burnt with fire with blackness & darkness & tempest and the sound of the trumpet and the voice of words giving forth the ten Commands which voice was so terrible intreated that the word Should not be spoken any more for they could not Endure that which was commanded & if so much as a beast touch the mountain it [^011-note1] was to be stoned or thrust through with a dart and so terrible was the sight that moses him. self said I exceedingly fear & quake: as tis said in the 18.19-20-21 verses. ~~ Here was a Great deal to detRestrain & deter the Chil of Israel from sin for they saw in what a dreadful manner the commands were given forth. They saw what a dreadful G. it was that gave those commands which might make em sensible how terrible his wrath would be against the breakers of the commands. But yet the apostle Intimates that their Fathers had not soso much to Restrain them from sin at that material earthly mountain Mt. Sinai as they have whobe are come to the bl Sp. Heavenly Mt Sion and theref the apostle on the after he had declared what theXtians were come to at mt Sion in the conclusion at the 25.26. verses he says ------------------------------

____________

In the three verses of the text the state that Xtians and circumstances of Xtians is described under a twofold Representation. 1. of a [^011-note2] Place that they are come to Here called mt Sion.. The City of the living G. the Heavenly Jerusalem. 2. The Inhabitants or persons there Resi dent ... To an innumerable Company of angels----------------------------

____________________________________________

The method on which I propose to discourse from these words is to shew in order in what sense Xtians are said to be come to Each of these . . 1. To show how they are come Xtians are come to mt Sion. 2. How they are come to the city of the loving G. the Hea venly Jerusalem. 3. how they are come to an Innumerable company of angels & so on so that Each particular may be taken as a distinct Proposition which I shall explain & Improve. & Theref.

_________________________________________

I. I would shew how Xtians may be said to be come to mt Sion.

__________________

Xtians being come to mt Sion denotes theseseveral tho they are ... kinds of things, that they are come to. & viz a Loud Sound. & founda. [^011-note3] a fortress. A Palace a Temple a throne & a Bu siness. _______________________________ In order to this I would shew where the literal mt Sion was of old. & then shew in the Second place how Xtians are come to mt Sion in a Spiritual Sense. The Literal mt Sion is that which was of old called mt Sion was a ---- -was a certain steep Rocky mountain or Hill in the City of Jerusalem. ~~ The City of Jerusalem was not built upon a plain being in hills. It was compassed about with many homes on the outside & not only so but the city itself was built on hills & or moun tains. ~~ There were formed Hills that it stood upon was & one of these was mt Sion upon the top of which stands there part of the city of Jerusalem that is called the City of David. ~~~ it was upon the top of this Hill that r David house or Palace stood. ~~ It was the Strongest part of all the City of Jeru salem. It seems to have been but little Else. And all high Solid Steep Rock of Difficult ascent & was a strong fortress & there p.5 fore it was the last part of the city of Jerusa lem .. that was taken out of the hands of the Jebusites and used to be called the strong hold & the fort. we have an account of David’s entry of it 2 Sam. 5.6 and onward. ----------- _______________________________________________________ ....... because David took it out of the hand of the Jebu sites & because his Palace stood there theref it was called the city of david as tis said in the 9 v. david built on the fort and Called it the City of David. ~~ and afterwards David brought up the ark there unto ... that Mt Zion or the City of David & placed in a Tabernacle that he had purchased for it . and have a particular account of it in the next Chap.

________________________________________

so that circumstance this mt Zion became the Place of the Sanctuary the place of God’s, more Immediate Residence & the Special Symbols of his Presence above all others parts of the Land of Canaan before that ~~ when the Chil. first came out of Egypt & settled in Canaan the ark was placed in Shiloh. But then it was taken captive by the Philistines & when it was returned out of the land of the Philistines [^011-note4] it was placed at Kiriath Jearim and till now G. had never revealed to say what place he had chosen to what city he had chosen to place his name there & therefore it is said that the ark wandered in a Temple & on a Tabernacle. ~~but now G. has Revealed to David that Jerusalem was the place that he had chosen. David went to fetch up the ark from Kiriath Jearim to place Jerusa lem but because Uzza was smitten it was left a while at the House of Obed edom & then afterwards was brought up to Jerusalem & was carried up unto Zion & placed or City of David & placed it in the Tabernacle that David had pitched for it there ... ever after 2. Sam. 6. 12 & e---------------- ------------------------. After this after the Middle of pg.8 I now proceed in the.

______________

Second Place. To shew what is denoted by Xtians being come to mt Sion. . It hereby seems to be signified these several things kinds of things they are brought to . a viz a Joyfull sound. & founda. a fortress & Palace a Temple a Throne a business 1. That denotes the joyfull sound that they hear as I have already offered the literal mt Sion as was a of old even a place where the silver>trumpet trumpet was blown being sounded by the Priests [^011-note5] to call the People to their solemn assemblies & in token of Joy at their Great festivals there was a Joyfull Sound that the Chil. of Is used to hear from mt Sion. So Xtians do spiritually hear the Joyfull sou Sound. they are brought to hear the sound sound of the Gospel trumpet. the sweet sound of its glorious doc. Its Gracious Invitat. & its Precious promises. The toapostle in the context put the Xtian Hebrews in mind what a horrible dreadfull sound their fathers heard at Mt. Sinai when they heard those awful thunders & that dreadfull trumpet that was sounded there exceeding Loud & such a terrible voice of words that all the People in the Camp trembled & Moses himself ----- the sound was so terrible to em that they could not En dure it the terribleness of it was too .. much for their feeble natures to hear they intreated they might hear it no more.

& But the apostle says we are not come to mt. Sinai to such a horrible have such a horrible sound but we are Come to mt. Zion where we may hear the pleasant sound from the silver trumpet of the Gosp. (gospel) [^011-note6]..... a sound that need not .... Fright us but may charm and & Re joice our hearts, tis not a sound that tends to drive us away with fear but a voice that tends to draw & invite us not a sound that we should need to desire that we may hear it no more but a voice that we may hear with the Greatest delight not the sounds of awfull threat enings as that was at Mt Sinai but that sound of the most Gracious promises. At Mt Sinai the Chil. of Is. heard the voice of G. as a Strict Lawgiver but Xtians are b are brought to hear the voice of X as a Savior . whose voice when the disciples heard it in his sermon on the mount was a pleasant voice not denouncing curses as the voice of mt Sinai did but pronouncing blessings, saying blessed are the -------------------------- 2. that It denotes the founda. that Xtians [^011-note7] are come to & that they build upon Unsure of section as I have observed before. much Mt Sion on which stands the City of David was a Rocky mountain so as to be little Else but a Solid Rock. ## 545. Heb. 12:22-24(b) 545.Heb. 12'22-24 edited

Jonathan Edwards

N. IId Vol. Heb 12: 22-24 April, 1740 I come now in the 2' Second Place to shew how Xtians are come to the Heavenly Jerusalem the City of the living G. the heavenly Jerusalem for these two expressions in the Text are put in opposition. The City of the ... as both signifying the same thing the City of the Living G. is the Heavenly Jerusalem. and Here. I would 1 shew what the L. said a temporal Jerusalem of old was . 2. shew Xtians are come unto the Heavenly Jersualem . 3.How that Heavenly Jerusalem is the City of the Living G . & then make some Improvement. . . . . . I. I would briefly shew where if? -------------------- It was the metropolis or chief city of all the Land of Israel it was the Royal City & the City where the Kings of the House of David dwelt it was the Chief City of the People of G. & it was a very Great & populous city & it had a Great multitude of Inhabitants & it was the Holy city it often had that Epithet given it- [^012-note1] Self & it was the City that G. chose out of all the tribes of Israel to place his name there the place to which where he app appointed the Temple to be built where....God’s ark & alter were placed. where all the alone it was Lawfule to offer Sacrifice & where all the males of the Chl. of Is. were obliged to Go & appear three times in the year. & it was called the city wheral G. of G. because there G. dwells between the Ch & the City of the Great King & the City of the Very G. to destroy with al Power the Cities of the Heathen round about them they looked upon Sacred as being dedicated to Jerusal dumb lifeless Idols as the Great City Babylon was the City where were the Temple of their Idol Bal. & so it was common among the Heathen their principal Cities were not only the Cities of their kings, but the cities where all their Chief Temples was built which were supposed to be the Chief Residences of their Gods who were lifeless Gods but [^012-note2] Jerusalem was the City of the living G.

-------------

and it was a very beautiful city High city built chiefly on mountains as were set before and Encompassed with Strong walls & many Towers of defence. walls about Jerusalem

----------------------

& it was a very magnificent beautifull city the buildings stately and set in excellent order. Ps 122.3. Jerusalem was is builded as a city compact together. & it was a City where God’s Peoples dwealt & Resided? & where they were sustained in the Lord and where all the principal Rulers and Judges of the Lord Resided. Ps. 122. - - -

### 546. Heb. 12:22-24(c)

### 546.Heb. 12'22-24(4_40) edited

Jonathan Edwards

Hebrews Sermon 4 Box 11. folder 834 III vol N Heb. 12.22.23.24-----------------------------To the General Assembly of the Chh of the firstborn which are written in Heaven. April 1740 I come now to shew in the FOURTH place How Xtians are said to come to the General Assembly & Chh of the firstborn which are written in Heaven. ------------- Repreaching notation in shorthand. II Doctrine preached second time 1741. That which is here called the General Assembly of all the ------------ is the whole Invisible Chh of X consisting both of living and departed Saints. ------ & containing not only both -- that part of the Chh. which is on Heaven--- the Earth and also that which is in Heaven. as in one word the whole mystical body of X. ~~~ There are many particular assemblies of Xtians multitudes of worshipping worshipping congregations of Gods People and there are nations provincial & national churches --- and there is the church universal, which in the ordinary explanation of the Phrase Signifies the whole Church militant upon Earth ~~~ But yet this don’t Contain the whole Chh of X made manifest--- & indeed is but a small part of the Chh in its Largest extent as consisting both of the Saints both in Heaven & Earth . and that is what is called the [^013-note1] Great Assembly of the Chh of the firstborn that are written in Heaven. Here several things come under consideration. 1. why this .. Chh of X in the sense of it is called an assembly.

-------------

2. Why it is called the Chh of the first born.

------------

3. what How they are said to be written in Heaven I. we are to consider why the Chh of X as Consisting of the saints both in Heaven & Earth is called an Assembly in Heaven & Earth is called an assembly. They are at a Great Distance one from another. The different parts of the Chh on Earth are very much separated. they are dispersed through Great part of the (world symb) among various nations, and hence -- they --- now assemble together into one place so far from that that many parts of the Chh of X on Earth never so much as knew of m many other parts of it. But these are not at so Great a dist. one from another as the Chh are Saints on Earth are different than the in Heaven are distant from the Saints on Earth. They dwell in a Θ that is Invisible and as an Inconceivable distance from us how then How then are they in Sp. all Spoken of as one Assembly as tho they were all assembled together. [^013-note2] It seems to be upon two Accounts. partly partly on account of their that assembly of them together which it was and partly an account of what shall be hereafter.

--------------------------------

1. They are now assembled together as a Spl. All assembled together in a Spiritual sense wholly th while they were in a natural state & under sin they were in a state of distance Separation from G. & distance one from another being alienated in their minds and Enemies both to G. & men and divided by contrary Interests. but in X Jesus G’ ___ they are all brought together. he hath Gathered them together in one. ~~~ They are all brought together as it were from the utmost Ends of the Earth under one head unto Jesus under one Head J.X. they are brought into his Kingdom & into his house. & have fellowship one with another. there is one G. one L. Jesus & one Sp. & they are called in one hope of their calling they in affection so far as they are known one to another. ~~~ Thus the Saints on Earth are one assembly with those in Heaven. they are all one an mystical body of X and all have fellowship in the same ------ the Spirit the same Business & the Same Inheritance. 2. Hereafter they shall all be often in a -literal litteral [^013-note3] sense be assembled together. They may The place of the assembly is in Heaven as the place of the assembly of Israel of old was the Temple.

-----------------

& that they are daily assembling going up every day & those that have not yet however There is a Great Assembly there already much the bigger Part of the Chh that is now in being is actually there and others are going there all of them are travelling that way and the time will soon come when they will all be together. They were not only ~~~ So that all the Saints left in Heaven & Earth are either actually assembled in the place of their general assembly or else are in their way thithernow so that they are all properly said to belong to the same assembly.

-----------------------------------

For Israel of old all the males above a certain age able went three times in the year to meet together in one assembly at the Temple & hence the whole nation were called the Congregation of the L of the L. or God’s assembly that & those members that staid at home were said to belong to this assemb the Congregation of Israel tho they had never yet assembled with others being yet in a State of Infancy. ~~~ So tis with that part of the Chh that is on Earth they they are as it were in a State of Infancy but [^013-note4] when that is past they shall be assembled in the Heavenly Teml Temple with the rest of the Congregation of Israel & then they shall abide together in that assembled state to all Eternity forever being together in the w of G. Heavenly worship tis an Assembly that never shall break up. II. place to consider why the Saints are called the first born. Exod. 24. 5 and he put young men of the Chil. of Is. which offered burnt off. & sacrificed Peace off. of oxen unto the L. These young men were doubly their first born sons who were the priests of Israel before the Separation of the Levites. And hence it may is -partly that Esaus act is folly his birthright his is _____ of as in Inheritance of......... his birthright which priesthood was a Sacred thing a priviledge which he ought ____ to leave untouchED from Regard to G as an holy thing and it shewed his lavish contempt of that which Sacred & Holy to sell it for a morsel of meat to satisfy a carnal appetite. --- Those were the priviledges of the first born of old had above other Children. From whence we may Suppose that the saints in the Text are called the first born in account of the position Priviledges which they have above the Rest of Gods Creatures. ................ [^013-note7] he then in the manner as he that had the birth right of old had position Priviledgdes above the Rest of the Chil. ~~~~~ God had been pleased to give the saints the birthright. th The Birthright of old did not always go according to priority of the real birth but some times, G. who is the Sove reign defender of all gave it to the youngest child. for the birth order by Gods appointment ~~~ Then he past the birthright to Isaac and appointed him to be Abrahams heir tho he was younger than Ishmael. he ordained that Hagar and Ishmael should be cast out to make a way for it.

2. Their names may are written in Heaven as they are enjoined among the Saints the citizens of Heaven. It has been the Custom in many Great Cities to keep Records of the names of their Citizens. or those that had a hope to be personages of the City So it was at Rome and so seems to have at Jerusalem. that Jesus Records of the names of the Jews that had any part in Jerusalem or any Right to come & worship here were written in the Rolls of that City & so may be Said to have a numeral in Jerusalem that Nehemiah tells Tobiah & Sanballat. Neh. 2.20. That they had no memorial in Jerusalem . . .. Their names were not written on the rolls of that city.

but attend with the di the utmost dilig. & dev. as setting yourself in the presence of G. & considering the House of G. as the Gate of Heaven. 2. Seeing you visibly belong to the Chh of the first born take heed that you don’t sell your birth right for a morsel of meat as Esau did The Apostle Paul gives this caution in the Context of those words that we have been insisting on this day 15.16.17. verses of the Chapter ---- [the ref. is in Hebrews 12] How Great was Esaus folly to sell such priviledges [^013-note20] as belonged so Great Priviledges as belonged to him as Isaacs first born Such as the Honour of the Priesthood and the Inheritance of the legacy of Abraham which Respected? not only his own life time But future Generations as well. don’t follow ... an Example of such Great folly & profaneness don’t sell the Priviledges that belong to the Chh of the first born for a moments pleasure when you are solicited by some eager sensu- [^013-note21] al appetite. or for a little worldly Gain which may well be compared to a morsel of meat. Don’t do that which is contrary to the commands of G. for such a trifle. ~~~ Consider the Consequences of Esaus folly his short momentary pleasure with his mess of pottage Cost him Long Sorrow & Repentance bitter crying & tears & all to no purpose. ~~~ don’t Gratify yourlusts for the present trusting to a Repentance hereafter mind what the apostle says concerning Esau to deter you from this folly for ye know how that afterward when he would have Inherited the blessing he was Rejected for he found no place of Repent . . ## 547. Heb. 12:22-24(d) 547.Heb. 12'22-24 edited

Jonathan Edwards

GEN MS 151 Box 11, folder 835 IV Vol. Heb. 12: 22. 23.24 ------- and to G. the Judge of all. --------- 1740 we are come now to the V. Particular in the Text. To shew how Xtians are come to G. the Judge of all . .

---------

and in order to this I would 1. shew the person is here Spoken of. 2. How he in a peculiar manner it said to be The Judge of all 3. How Xtians are said to be come to him as This Judge of all. Copy on Trinity ----- I. It may be enquired who this is that is here Spoken of. The words thems. shew that tis God that is Spoken of but that don’t fully Resolve the Question for there are more persons than one that are G. G. the F. & Jesus & the Holy Ghost are Each of them called G. in SS.

-------------

& sometimes when G. is Spoken of as one parti cular person in the Godhead it intended more to another. But what is intended is the deity or the divine nature common to all that all the persons in the Trinity & .. all the Persons are Included. ~~~ But when G. is spoken of in this place tis not the deity or the divine Essence as common to all the Persons that seem to be intended all the Persons of the Trinity are not included. But the answer [^014-note1] to the Enq. is that -

--------

G. the F. is the person Spoken of and is here said to be the Judge of all tis not the Son nor the Holy G. nor the deity as including all the persons an but the Farther particularly & this is manifest by the distinction that is used in the Text between G. the Judge & Jesus the mediator. ~~~ in the text Here .. Xions are said to be come to G. the Judge of all & in the next verse but one they are said to .. be come to J. the mediator of the new covenant which makes it manifest that they are true disinct persons that are Spoken of. And the very last distinction ... & the mutual Relation of the two offices makes is manifeste that they are two distinct persons that are Spoken one of them a being the office or being the Part of Judge and the other acting as mediator before that Judge. Which shews the Person that is called the Judge of all to be G. the F. ..... for he is the Judge before whom X appears as [^014-note2] Mediator. He is the Judge that of Lawgiver & Judge that Required Satisfaction to be made to the Law that appeal shew expects a Righ. that shall answer the Law & he is the Judge that to whom this satisfaction is & Righteousness is presented & him who accepts of it & he is the Person that .. acquits & Justifies the Sinner in account of X’ satisfaction & Righ. For tis the part of a Judge to accept a satisfaction to a Law and tis the part as a Judge ... to Justi fy a person as having answered that Law and being Righ. according to the Law. but this way to matter may be more fully explained under the next head viz that II. Thing that was proposed which is to shew how G. the F. in a particular manner is said to be the Judge of all and the reason then G. the ... as distinguished from the other Persons of the Trinity. In order the more fully to understand this the following things may be noted.

---------------------

1. among the Three persons of the Trinity Each one has his part & distinct office ... [^014-note3] as it were his distinct office assigned him by agreement among themselves. That There is that which the SS Speaking of G. after the manner of men represents as an agreement one a Consultation and agreement among the Persons of the Trinity. .... Respecting the divine ... acts & disposals towards men. Reasonable Creatures. ..... assigning to Each one his part. in the management as or distinct office in the management or ______ tions of divine Providence towards them ........fallen men thus in Zech.6.13. the Counsel of Peace is said to be between the F. & the Son. ~~~ That disposition of things among the Persons of the Trinity wheref. Each one has his distinct office is what divines commonly call the Economy of the Persons of the Trinity.

_____________________________________

2. By this agreement the Place or office of G. the Father is to be the giver the defend- & Judge of the Law. G. the F. by the same agreement as Crea. of all the Reborn is the person that is the maintain the Right. of the Godless. that is to be his personal [^014-note4] office .. maintain the authority & majesty of the deity ~~~ & theref. it belongs to him .. especially to be the Lawgiver & to ... defend the divine Law to see to it that the authority of the Law be defended ~~~& .. do his peculiar office not only to give the Law but as he .... has the care of its defence he is the Judge He is the Person that is to have the Care of avenging Rebellion against the Law & the dishonour done to it ~~~ & it belongs to him to Judge when the Law is answered as satisfied & to accept the Satisfaction.

------------------------

& therf when X as mediator undertakes to ... make satisfaction to the Law he made the Satisfaction to him the Father the Father was the person to whom it belonged to Require the Satisfaction. & to accept it because he was ........... the Oerson whose office it was appointed to be the defender & Judge of the Law ~~~ other wise it would have been no more proper that the satisfaction would be offered to G. the P. than to G. the Son or the H.Gh. deleted two lines [^014-note5] As the office of the F. was to be the Person defender & Judge of the Law so the office that the Son was appointed to was to be the mediator between G. & the Sinners that had violated the Law. and the office of the Holy Gh. was to be the ~~~~ He by appoint ment was to be a person that in his office was to act under the F. as being appointed by him & interceding before him as paying a price to him & seeking acceptance of him.

----------------

The office of the H.Gh. was to be the Emissary or messenger of the other Persons. tis in his office to act under both the others to Execute what the others that which may is made by the other two in that office to accomplish the sucess of the negotiations that are between the F. & the Son.

These several distinct parts & offices, that here are undertaken by each person of the Trinity as mentioned according according to that covenant which they have Entered into from Et. ~~~ & by this Cov. .. tis peculiarly the place of the F to be Judge of all. &

it is as Judge that the mediator appears before is was as Judge of the Law that [^014-note6] G. the F. required satisfaction & it was as Judge that he accepted the satisfaction that X made. & it It was as Judge that he Justified X and acquitted & Rewarded him after he had made full satiscation for Sin & it is as Judge of the Law that G. the Father aquitt acquits & Justified sinners that believe in X. the Justification is an act that peculiarly ~~~ Redemption belongs to the Son but Justification peculiarly belongs to the office of G. the F. X workt out a Righ. for sinners, but tis G. the F. that Justifies em for the Righ. & ~~~ but Justification is properly the act of the Judge is an act of a Judge .. Justifying and Condemning are Judicial acts or the acts of a Judge : 3. It may be objected that G. is the Judge of all neither of the other of the Persons of the Trinity are. In Giving X to fulfill the Law for us G. asks & in unity a new Cov. with men G. acts as a Sovereign but these acts of Sovreignty don’t at all prejudice his character as a [^014-note10] strict Judge of the Law because he does some thing herein that the Law did not direct to yet he does nothing contrary to the Law in it . ~~~ but after X is given and has full filled the Law G. in bestowing the blessings that X has purchased still acts as a Strict Judge of the Law he in Justifying & Rewarding believers in X does as much do the part of a Strict JUDGE OF THE Law as in Eternally Condemning Sinners whom G. hath set forth.

to declare Glory at this time his R. that he might be Just & the Justtification of him that believes on Jesus.

Tho he don’t there sit only on a Throne of Justice as he did at Mt Sinai but also on a Throne of Grace. 2. others that are under the Law can come & have access to the Strict Judge of all after this manner for G. is a Consuming Fire to them that are under the Law. ~~~~ This is a privildge that persons are brought to no where else but at Mt Sion. The Chil. of Israel could not not come night to this Strict Judge [^014-note12] At Mt Sinai for as the apostle observes in the 20. v. of the Context if so much as a beast touch the mountain he must be stoned or thrust through with a dart. 3. Tho Xtians come to G. as a Judge yet tis their peculiar priviledge not to come to him as a Lawgiver. as man did under the first law. Xtians or believers in X appear before G. as a Judge as much as Ever & G. acts as a Judge of the Law in Justifying & Rewarding them for the Righ. of X but they don’t come to G. as a Lawgiver so they are brought before G. as a Judge as much as unbeleivers that have no interest in the Cov. of Grace. ~~~ but they don’t appear before G. as a Lawgiver in the same sense that men did under the first Cov. for them G. Gave man the Law to be fulfilled by for him to fulfill and at mt Sinai the Law was given to men that are under the Law as exacting it of them to fulfill it thems. upon pain of damnation was the Law at Mt Sinai given. to Xtians at Mt Sinai is exacting a [^014-note13] fulfillment of them Because X has already fulfilled it for them. X has already appeared before G as a Lawgiver for them & they now only come to him as a Judge to Receive the Reward of his obedience. Application. Here is matter of Great consolation to Real Xtians & Terrour & Conviction to others 1. Here is Just Ground of Great Consolation & Rejoicing to true Xtians. What has been said shews the sure Grounds that Xtians hopes of happiness are built upon. There is Every way Ground for Strong Consolation unto them There is Great Consolation that they may fetch from the Throne of Grace that G. sits on on Mt Sion & not only that there is also Strong Consolation to be derived from the throne of Justice that G. also sits upon there. ~~~ not only the Infinite mercy of G. but also the Strict immoveable & Immutable Justice of G. is for them. both Law & Gospel will unite withall for their happiness & Eternal Glory. [^014-note14]

----------------

The founda. of their blessedness can no more be shaken than Gods Throne can be broken. yea both his Thrones his Throne of Grace & his Throne of Judgement may as soon be overthrown as their blessedness in he. overthrown. ~~~ we may be sure that Gods Throne is settled first on a Strong foundation it is said that tis Established of old. Ps. 93.2. Thy Throne Established of old. & the Xtians blessedness is settled in a foundation Equally Strong and Immovable. If G. had only revealed that his mercy was infinite & sufficient to bestow blessedness on the Saints & had promised it on that Ground that would have been sure ground of hope but that the heirs of promise may have more strong Consolation & firm hope not only Gods mercy & truth but his Justice as a Strict Judge are revealed to be on their side. It may well Remove all their doubts [^014-note15] & sins to consider that they are not come to mt Sinai to Receive the Law as Enjoined upon them & a perfect observance of it exacted of them because X has been to mt Sinai for them & has Received the Law & has fulfilled it & all that Remains for them is only to come to G. as a Judge and to be acquitted & Justified & Rewarded by him as Such For the obedience that is already wrought out. It may Justly be matter of Great Rejoicing to you that tho G. be so Strict a Judge yet there is way made that you may have access to him may be accepted of him & may be blessed in him forever & that tho you have so Greatly Sin’d against him. It may be matter? of Rejoicing to you that G. does sit on a throne of Strict Justice as he does & that he is so Holy & Right a Judge. For your happiness is so much the more sure for it. [^014-note16] 2. what has been said may Justly suggest matter of terror to all that are not True Xtians. For when you hear that Xtians are come to G. the Judge of all you have to Consider also.

------------------------------------

That tis impossible you should have any Friendly access to or favour with this Great & Strict Judge in the State that you are in. G. will be a Consuming fire to all such as you on his Throne of Judgment he will burn up all that come night with fire unquenchable. ~~~ There he appeared as a Consuming fire at Mt Sinai the Mt was altogether on a smoke & it burnt such a terrible manner that the flames ascended up as tis said even to the midst of Heaven. Deut.4.11. how can you come to G. in Mt Sinai which all around encompassed with fierce lightenings and has bounds set about which if any man broke through the Lord would brake forth upon [^014-note17] Him & Consume yea if so much as a beast touch the mount he -------- how much more shall a guilty provoking Rebel be thrust through with the darts of divine vengeance. & you so consider that tho you but have acceptance with such a Judge yet you must have to do with him you but Get out of his hands you must stand before this Great Judge & how dreadfull will his holiness & Justice & almighty Power be to you then can your hands be strong or ------------ who shall stand when he appears he will be as a Refiners fire -------- The day of the L. shall burn as an oven & all the wicked of the Earth shall be as Stubble.

you little conceive how dismal a thing it will be to stand before such a Judge in your sins & with the guilt of all the Sins that even you have Committed you little Conceive how dismal astonishment & those heart shrinkings & horrible amazement will be that will [^014-note18] then seize upon you O how Ready will you be hide in the caves & dens. ---------------- ---------- but how will all be in vain? when you must stand before his Judgment Seat & must Receive the awfull sent out his mouth & must bear the Eternal Execution of it. 3. This may be matter of conviction to Sinners of their necessity of a mediator in order to their Access to G. If G. not only sat on Sinai but also sits on Mt Sion as a Strict Judge as on a throne of Strict Justice . Then doubtless we can never have access to him but through one that has fulfilled and satisfied the Law. & tis in work for you to hope to have access & acceptance by your own Righ. [-] dont you Rest in past attainmts but is the the breathing & struggle of your soul after an higher degree to be more deliv. from sin this body of sin [-] & death this Heavy ---- of a sinfull Heart that you serve G. more freely & with more [--] Life & ardency of sp & may live more to his Glory and are you thus as the apostle was who forgot the things towards the mark for the prize of the high calling of G. which is in X Jesus you should be strict in your Examination of your [^015-note23] selves in these things as it Greatly Concerns you to Know what society you be a part you belong lest herefater [-] you be amazingly disappointed when your spirit Comes to be separated [-] from your body & when you expected to Go to the sp of Just men made perfect you unexpectedly find your self among the damned spirits in prison the damned spirits of wicked men mad
